NOTICE TO THE SHAREHOLDERS
NOTICE is hereby given that the 24th Annual General Meeting (AGM) of the Shareholders of M/s. Rail Infrastructure Development  
Company (Karnataka) Limited will be held on   20.12.2025 at 1.00 PM at Room No.313, 3rd Floor, Vidhana Soudha, Bengaluru 
– 560001 to transact the following business: -
ORDINARY BUSINESS 
1.  Adoption of Financial Statements: 
 To receive, consider and adopt:
(a) The Audited Standalone Financial Statements of the Company for the Financial Year ended March 31, 2025 together with the Re -
ports of the Board of Directors and the Auditors thereon; and
(b) The Audited Consolidated Financial Statements of the Company for the Financial Year ended March 31, 2025, together with the 
Report of the Auditors thereon.
 together with the comments received from C&AG under section 143 of the Companies Act, 2013.
 To consider, and, if thought fit, to pass with or without modification(s), the following resolution as an Ordinary Resolution:
 “RESOLVED THAT, the financial statements of the company for the period from 1st April 2024 to 31st March 2025, together with all 
the Schedules, notes   annexed thereto, tabled at the Meeting, be and are hereby approved.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT, the reports of the Board of Directors and Auditors (Statutory and Secretarial) along with the certificate 
of CAG (Comptroller and Auditor General)  be and are hereby adopted. ”
2. To fix the remuneration of the statutory auditors appointed by the C&AG of India for the Financial year 2025-26 :
 To consider, and, if thought fit, to pass with or without modification(s), the following resolution as an Ordinary Resolution:
 “RESOLVED THAT, pursuant to the provisions of Sections 139(7) and 142 and all other applicable provisions of the Companies 
Act, 2013 and the Rules framed thereunder, and as amended from time to time, and such other permissions as may be necessary 
read with the Companies (Audit and Auditors) Rules, 2014, the payment of the remuneration of 4,00,000/-  PA(Rupees Four Lakhs 
Only) plus with applicable Statutory Taxes to M/s P K Subramaniam & Co,Chartered Accountants, Bengaluru [Firm Registration 
No. 004135S) who were appointed by the Comptroller and Auditor General (C&AG) CA.V/COY/KARNATAKA, RIDCKL (1)/590 dated 
11.09.2025 as Statutory Auditors to conduct the audit of the financial records maintained by the Company for period from 1st April 
2025 to 31st March 2026 be and is hereby approved. ”
SPECIAL BUSINESS:
3.  To Consider the appointment of Mr. LAKSHMAN SINGH  (DIN: 11417363), as Managing Director of the company and fix the 
remuneration payable to him:
 EXPLANATORY STATEMENT PURSUANT TO SECTION 102 OF THE COMPANIES ACT, 2013 IN RESPECT OF SPECIAL BUSINESS
 APPOINTMENT OF MANAGING DIRECTOR
 “RESOLVED THAT pursuant to section 196(4) and other applicable provisions of the Companies Act 2013, the members of the 
Company in the Twenty Fourth Annual General Meeting of the Company do hereby approve, confirm and ratify the appointment 
of  Mr. Lakshman Singh, IRSE as the Managing Director. 
 Date  of   :   01.12.2025
 Appointment
 Designation   :    Managing Director
 Tenure                   :     3 years from the date of appointment or until further orders  from the appointing authority.
 Powers and Duties  :    To look after the whole and substantially whole of the affairs  of the Company including day to day  
         affairs, subject to control, supervision and superintendence of the Board of  Directors. 
 Emoluments     :     Salary and perquisites applicable to a Group A officer of  the Railway Board, GOI as set out in the Board 
          Resolution No 03/BOD-112/2025-26 in the 112th Board Meeting heldon 20.12.2025.

RAIL INFRASTRUCTURE DEVELOPMENT 
COMPANY (KARNATAKA) LIMITED
To, 
The Members
DIRECTORS REPORT
[Pursuant to Section 134 of the Companies Act 2013 read with Companies (Accounts) Rules 2014]
Your directors have pleasure in presenting their Twenty- Third Annual Report along with the audited statement for the year 
ended 31st March 2024, Balance Sheet as on that date and Cash Flow Statement, together with Auditors’ Report thereon.
1. PRELIMINARY:
 Based on the directives of the Ministry of Railways, the Company was restructured as a State Joint Venture with equity 
participation between Government of Karnataka and Ministry of Railways at 51:49 ratio vide Revised Shareholders 
Agreement dated 17.10.2018.  In view of restructuring of K-RIDE as a State JV, the activities/scope of the Company has 
been widened which amongst others, include the following: 
a) development, financing and implementation of railway projects and/or projects which may require viability gap funding 
that are important for critical connectivity/ capacity enhancement, as agreed by the Parties;
b) undertaking surveys, preparation of Detailed Project Report (DPR) to examine the viability of the railway projects;
c) processing for sanctioning of the railway projects (identified by the Board or the parties); 
d) incorporation and funding of subsidiaries/special purpose vehicles for undertaking railway projects; and
e) Undertaking all such actions not specifically mentioned above, that may be required to give effect to the intent of the 
Parties, as set out in the Agreement.
2. FINANCIAL HIGHLIGHTS:
 The financial results for the year ended March 31, 2025 are summarised below.

What’s in a Name ?
The 4 BSRP corridors are named
after 4 regional flowers
SAMPIGE
(Champa)
Corridor - 1
MALLIGE
(Chameli)
Corridor - 2
PARIJAATA
(Prajakta)
Corridor - 3
KANAKA
(Priyadarsha)
Corridor - 4
And we called it
Meaning ‘Connectivity’ in Kannada
BSRP aims to connect Bengaluru city to its suburbs and satellite townships and will carry 
about 9.84 lakhs passengers per day upon completion thus, decongesting the road traffic of 
Bengaluru city.
Foundation Stone for BSRP project laid by Hon’ble Prime Minister on 20th June2022, with an 
assurance to complete the project in 40 months. K-RIDE is country’s most integrated, state -of-
the-art, suburban rail initiative and also the first under the National Suburban Rail Policy -2018. 
KRIDE is working hard to meet this challenge.
1. Length:  BSRP project envisages 4 corridors with a total route length of 148.17 Kms with 
57 nos of Stations and 2 Maintenance depots
Corridors Section Length  
(in Kms)
1 Bengaluru - Devanahalli + Airport Connectivity 41.40
2 Baiyyappanahalli – Chikkabanavara 25.01
3 Kengeri – Whitefield 35.52
4 Heelalige – Rajanukunte 46.24
Total 148.17
2. Project inclusion   :    Bengaluru Sub-urban Rail project has been sanctioned by Govt of India 
   through Ministry of Railways on 21.10.2020

cost is

the possibility of procurement of trains through ICF, Chennai.

20. RISK MANAGEMENT POLICY:
Although the company has long been following the principle of risk minimization as is the norm in every industry, it has 
now become a compulsion.
The main objective is to ensure sustainable business growth with stability and to promote a pro-active approach in  
reporting, evaluating and resolving risks associated with the business. 
The common risks inter alia are: Regulations, competition, Business risk, Technology obsolescence, Investments,  
Business risk, inter-alia, further includes financial risk, political risk, fidelity risk, legal risk.
As a matter of policy, these risks are assessed and steps as appropriate are taken to mitigate the same.

Independentstatutory auditors of the company for the F.Y 2024-25.
Auditors’ Report:
The qualifications in the Auditors Report under “Basis for qualified opinion” for the financial year ended 31.03.2025 have 
been suitably replied by the management. Details of the Management replies for the Observations of the Statutory  
Auditor on the Standalone, and Consolidated Financial Statement and alsoInternal Financial Controls over Financial  
Reporting on the Consolidated Financial Statement of K-RIDE for the year ended 31-March-2025are as under: 
Management’s Replies to the Observations of the Statutory Auditor on the  
 Standalone  Financial Statement of K-RIDE for the year ended 31-March-2025
Sl.No. BASIS OF QUALIFIED OPINION MANAGEMENT REPLIES
1
The K-RIDE is mainly involved in execution of Railway Line 
Doubling Projects and was operating the funds related to the 
same. However, during last two financial years the Company 
started receiving funds from Government of Karnataka (Here-
inafter called GOK) as well as Ministry of Railways (Hereinafter 
called MOR) for implementation of Bangalore Sub-urban Rail-
way Project (Herein after called BSRP Project). Again, as per di-
rections received, the KRIDE has established BRIDE as its sub -
sidiary to execute BSRP Project. Meanwhile the funds received 
from GOK and MOR towards BSRP Project; despite having 
created a subsidiary for execution of BSRP Project, the com -
pany has converted the funds received from GOK and MOR 
into Share Capital of the Company. Even though the KRIDE is  
executing the BSRP Project, the discussions are going on to 
transfer entire BSRP funds and corresponding assets and li -
abilities to BRIDE. There is no clarity either from GOK or MOR 
regarding this issue. By allotting shares to MOR the Company 
has made MOR shareholder to the extent of 49 % of its exist -
ing operations which was not warranted. This has resulted 
into doubt regarding continuity of BSRP Project Implementa-
tion through KRIDE. This has a bearing on Accounting issues, 
Shareholding issues and Going Concern issues etc. In the ab -
sence of clarity, we are not in a position to quantify the Finan-
cial and other Implications on the Company.
Due to Administrative reason, K-RIDE continued its 
business with respect to implementation of Bengal -
uru Suburban Rail Project (BSRP)/Bengaluru subur -
ban Transport Project (BSTP) and receiving the funds 
through respective Governments (i.e from GoK&MoR 
respectively) based on the funding pattern sanctioned 
by both the Governments. (i.e. 51% GoK& 49% MoR)
However, in the 110th Board Meeting it has been  
resolved to transfer all Assets and Liabilities with re -
spect to BSRP/BSTP Project from K-RIDE to Bi-RIDE 
which has been constituted for the exclusive purpose 
of implementation and management of BSRP/BSTP . 
The whole process will be completed within next Six-
nine months. The necessary actions are under process.
Accordingly, the continuity of the BSRP business is not 
impacted, as the project along with all its related as -
sets, liabilities, contractual obligations, rights, and re -
sponsibilities is being transferred to Bi-RIDE as a going 
concern, in accordance with the approval of the Board 
and the directions of the Government.
Till the completion of the transfer process, K-RIDE  
continues to discharge its obligations relating to 
BSRP/BSTP and necessary accounting treatment shall 
be carried out in accordance with applicable account-
ing standards and statutory requirements.
Also, it is imperative to note that Doubling Projects 
will be continued to be executed by K-RIDE.

[page 22]
24
TH
 ANNUAL REPORT 2024-25
22
Page         of 162
2
The Company is executing two Railway line doubling projects 
of Southwestern Railways (SWR in brief ). The company is fol -
lowing percentage of completion method based on cost in -
curred method/ Input method under Ind AS for recognition 
of Revenue. Since the company is using Input method, it is 
prudent to recognize Revenue based on the estimated cost as 
on date. However, the company is recognizing Revenue based 
on the approved cost dated 14-07-2022. As per MOU dated 
14.07.2022 between The Company and SWR any change in 
the DPR and the detailed estimate shall be sanctioned by SWR. 
The company has submitted a detailed revised estimated cost 
based on current rates and duly approved by the technical 
team to MOR. Following are the differences between original 
estimates and revised estimates.
“As per information provided to us, till date revised estimated 
cost is not approved by SWR. In the absence of revised ap -
proved estimated cost, we are unable to quantify the impact 
on Revenue and Profit thereon. 
This matter was also reported in the previous audit report for 
the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved. ” 
Revenue is recognized based on the approved sanc -
tioned estimate (MOU signed on 14.07.2022 i.e. FY 
2022-23) & the same has been disclosed in notes to 
accounts. However, K-RIDE has worked out the revised 
estimate considering escalation costs and change in 
GST rate which has been submitted to SWR for ap -
proval. Further, after approval of SWR &GoK, the same 
will be considered accordingly.
3 The Net Profit for the reporting period is Rs.1031.30 lakhs, 
which includes Bank Interest income of Rs.1,577.52 lakhs. 
Company being a Implementing Agency, has to receive a 
fixed percentage of Supervision Charges and cannot incur op-
erational loss during the normal course of business. However, 
barring Interest Income, the company has incurred a loss be -
fore taxes amounting to Rs.546.22 lakhs. The Main reason for 
the loss is that the Company has submitted a detailed revised 
Estimate duly approved from its Technical Team for approval 
before the Ministry of Railway Authorities. As on date of sign -
ing this report, the Ministry of Railways has not approved the 
revised estimate. This has resulted into Understatement of 
Revenue as well as Receivable Balance. In absence of com -
plete information, we are not in a position to quantify the ex -
tent of Short Revenue and corresponding Receivables value. 
This may also create Ind AS issues in subsequent financial 
years as Ind AS requires that any adjustments made in books 
corresponding to previous financials years, then correspond -
ing effect shall be given to those financial years and there will 
be a need to restate the previous financial years figures.
Revenue is recognized based on the approved sanc -
tioned estimate/DPR (MOU signed on 14.07.2022 i.e. 
FY 2022-23) & the same has been disclosed in notes to 
accounts. However, K-RIDE has worked out the revised 
estimate considering escalation costs and change in 
GST rate which has been submitted to SWR for ap -
proval. Further, after approval of SWR &GoK, the same 
will be considered accordingly. 
As diclosed in the financials note no.32(a), All the ex -
penses pertaining to BSRP including apportioned 
cost have been transferred to capital work in pro -
gress whereas, Doubling/ Admin & general overhead 
expenses are charged to P&L account. (i.e. Expendi -
ture pertaining to support departments such as Fi -
nance, HR, IT, Admin & general overhead expenses are 
charged to P&L account.)
The Railway Line doubling project on specified routes are be-
ing undertaken by KRIDE in joint participation between Min -
istry of Railways (MOR in brief ) and Government of Karnataka 
(GOK in brief ). Both MOR and GOK have contributed to the 
project in Equal Proportion. The Modus operandi is that the 
KRIDE will raise Invoice to Ministry of Railways as per agreed 
terms and conditions. Against these invoices the funds will 
have to be received both from MOR and GOK.

4
Even though GOK is contributing 50% of its shares all the Bill-
ings by KRIDE shall be made to MOR as per terms of agree -
ment. As per information provided to us the GOK will hand 
over money to KRIDE directly. MOR will release its share as 
and when the Bills are raised by the KRIDE. As a matter of pru-
dence and to reflect the true and fair view of the affairs of the 
company, the company has to account for contract revenue as 
per the terms of the agreement entered between the compa-
ny, MOR and GOK. As each of the participants contribute 50% 
of the funds required for the project, ideally the receivables 
balance of the company should reflect how much money is 
receivable from GOK as well as MOR. 
As can be seen from Accounts MOR has released its share as 
and when the bills are raised. However, no written Balance 
Confirmation is obtained from either GOK or MOR with re -
spect to Contract Revenue Accounted, Funds Released and 
balance receivable. For KRIDE, MOR and GOK are like outside 
customers, and KRIDE is not bound by budget allocations of 
GOK. In the normal course of business, company has to ac -
count for revenue as per IndAS 115 and the receivables from 
each of the customer has to be reflected in the books of ac -
counts. As per the information provided by the company, the 
status of work executed and funds received/receivable in re -
spect of doubling projects as at 31st March 2025 is as under:
In our view the MOR has released excess money, and the GOK 
has to pay its dues to KRIDE. It is also not clear whether the 
excess amount received from MOR is advance received or to 
be recognized as revenue. As per statement furnished the 
GOK has given 10000 Lakhs as on date and the balance re -
ceivable is Rs. 25,336 Lakhs. Presuming, MOR has released 
Rs.8,147 Lakhs in excess, company has to show advance re -
ceived from MOR and corresponding balance to be shown as 
25,336 Lakhs. This has affected the true and fair presentation 
of the Accounts as Current assets have been understated by 
25,336 Lakhs and Current liabilities have been understated by 
Rs.8,147 Lakhs.
Funds will be released based on the Budget earmarked 
(for the particular year and availability of funds). How-
ever, it is to note that GoK has released only Rs.100 
Crore during the FY 2020-21 and no further funds 
made available from GoK for doubling projects. A 
continuous effort are being made by K-RIDE to get 
the necessary funds from GoK by communicating it 
through official letters sighting the shortage of funds.
As a temporary arrangement, K-RIDE has utilised the 
BSRP funds to the extent of Rs.171.89 Crore for Dou -
bling project to meet the work target and contractual 
obligations as both the projects are equally funded by 
GoK&MoR.
The same will be recouped to BSRP project on receipt 
of Doubling funds from GoK.
The receivable will only be accounted upon issue of 
Government Order (GO) for release of funds. (since, the 
release of funds will depend on availability of funds 
with the Government and based on the different pa -
rameters even though the budget is earmarked).
Accordingly, no receivable shall be recognised merely 
on the basis of budget allocation; recognition will be 
made only when there is reasonable assurance evi -
denced by a GO sanctioning the release in the particu-
lar year.
5
Further since GOK has not paid its dues the amount payable 
to contractor should have gone up to that extent. But on our 
verification, it was observed that the Funds received from 
GOK in respect of BSRP project has been used to pay to the 
Contractors and as a result there is no outstanding dues pay -
able to Contractors.  In order to make payments to contractors 
out of earmarked funds for BSRP Projects, the Company man-
agement should have obtained prior permission from GOK as 
the funds are allocated out of specified budgetary allocations 
from GOK and since it is approved in Karnataka legislative As-
sembly and project specific funds are received, there cannot 
be diversion of funds from one project to another project. This 
has resulted into violation of financial powers vested with the 
company resulting in diversion of funds without written ap -
proval from GOK to the extent of Rs.25,336 Lakhs.

7
The Company has not ascertained the liability for Property Tax 
under The Bruhat Bengaluru MahanagaraPalike Act, 2020 on 
lands acquired by the Company. During the year no provision 
for property tax is made by the Company. We are unable to 
quantify the impact on profit.
This matter was also reported in previous audit report for the 
year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
Property Tax can be assessed on completion of Katha 
transfer in the name of K-RIDE. The process of trans -
ferring property accounts of the acquired assets to K-
RIDE is currently in progress. 
On completion of Katha transfer the Property Tax will 
be identified by Land department and necessary pro -
visions will be made in books.
Due to a recent software update at the BBMP office, 
the process of account transfer has temporarily been 
impacted, and further transfers are currently not pos -
sible. However, the remaining cases are under active 
processing, and all necessary steps are being taken to 
expedite khata registration.
In this regard, a letter dated 03.02.2025 has been is -
sued by the Managing Director of K-RIDE to the Special 
Commissioner (Revenue), BBMP , requesting suitable 
modifications in the system to facilitate a smoother 
and faster khata transfer process in favor of 
K-RIDE.
8
The Company is availing GST input tax credit in respect of  
direct expenditure relating to doubling project and is not 
availing Input Tax Credit relating to BSRP project under the 
pretext that it is not entitled for credit as per provisions  
section 17(5) of the CGST/KGST Act. However, since the BSRP 
Project is going to be transferred to a separate entity in our 
view the Company is Supplying Works Contract to Ministry of 
Railways and is incurring the Capital Expenditure in fiduciary 
capacity out of funds received from MOR and GOK. Therefore, 
the Company could have claimed Input Tax Credit. Further the 
input tax credit on common expenditure allocated to dou -
bling projects is not claimed and the same is debited to Profit 
and Loss statement. In absence of information, we are unable 
to quantify the impact on profit. The Input in respect of BSRP 
Project amounts to Rs.3,365.57 Lakhs. 
This matter was also reported in previous audit report for the 
year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
Currently K-RIDE is implementing two Doubling  
projects along with BSRP project. K-RIDE has claimed 
GST input specifically related to Doubling project and 
inputs related to BSRP project are not claimed since 
the same is blocked u/s 17(5) of CGST Act, 2017.  In -
puts related to BSRP projects along with the expens -
es have been included in the Capital WIP for which  
depreciation will be claimed as per provisions of sec -
tion 16(3) of CGST Act, 2017 as and when the asset is 
ready for its intended use.
K-RIDE has not claimed GST inputs on common ex -
penditure allocated to doubling project based on 
the expert opinion taken from the Tax Consultant of 
K-RIDE.

9
The Company has shown the opening balance of Rs.16,541.82 
Lakhs (net of transfer to share capital) as Interest Free Subordi-
nate Debt from GOK (Advance from GOK - BSRP Project-Land 
Acquisition). During the current financial year the Company 
has received an additional amount of Rs.59,000 Lakhs as an 
Advance from GOK - BSRP Project-Land Acquisition. As per let-
ter dated 05-12-2024 from GOK, it is mentioned that the funds 
of Rs.16,541.82 Lakhs should be treated as the Subordinate 
debt. For the balance funds of Rs. Rs.59,000 Lakhs, no confir -
mation received from GOK either to treat it as a Subordinate 
debt or grant-in-aid. 
In our opinion the aforesaid amount of Rs.59,000 Lakhs relat -
ing to Advance from GOK for Land Acquisition shall be shown 
as Government Grants for Capital Expenditure, as per Ind AS 
20- Deferred Government Grants Para 12 and 13 relating to 
Accounting for Government Grants. This has resulted in un -
derstatement of Deferred Government Grants and overstate -
ment of Long-Term Borrowings by Rs.17,101.25 Lakhs and 
other equity by Rs.41,898.75 Lakhs and also resulted in non-
compliance to Division II of Schedule III to the Companies 
Act,2013.
As per the approved Financing pattern with respect 
to Bengaluru Suburban Rail Project (BSRP) in line with 
Government Order from GoK vide GO No. UDD 27 PRJ 
2020, Bengaluru dated 07.07.2020 and Sanction order 
from Ministry of Railways (Railway Board) vide OM 
no.2019/JV Cell/KRIDE/BSTP/EBR/07 dated 21.10.2020, 
the cost of the Land and R&R for BSRP has to be borne 
by GoK through Sub-ordinate Debt. 
Since, KRIDE has received the Funds on account of 
Land Acquisition, the same has been accounted in 
KRIDE’s Books of account as Sub-ordinate debt (Long 
Term Borrowings) based on the sanctioned funding 
pattern.
10
The Company has received Rs.50,000 lakhs from MOR towards 
Share Application Money up till 31.03.2024. Out of the aggre-
gate amount of Rs.50,000 lakhs, The Company has transferred 
Rs.48,039.22 lakhs to Share Capital during the financial year 
2023-24 and the balance of Rs.1,960.79 Lakhs has shown as 
Grant from MOR – BSRP under Long Term Borrowings.
In our opinion, the aforesaid balance of Rs.1,960.79 Lakhs shall 
be shown as Share Application Money pending allotment. This 
has resulted in understatement of Share Application Money 
pending allotment and overstatement of Long-Term Borrow-
ings by Rs. 1,960.79 Lakhs and also resulted in non-compli -
ance to Division II of Schedule III to the Companies Act,2013.
This matter was also reported in previous audit report for the 
year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
In the 97th Board Meeting it was resolved that the al -
lotment of shares for equity investments under BSRP 
by GOK & MOR in K-RIDE shall be in ratio of 51:49 as 
per the stipulations contained in the AOA of K-RIDE & 
to avoid disproportionate shareholding for equity un-
der BSRP . It was also resolved that the GOK’s 50% share 
of equity under BSRP released from time to time under 
BSRP shall be treated as 51% equity and the propor -
tionate 49% of MOR’s equity will be worked out from 
the 50% share of MOR’s equity under BSRP released 
from time to time and the balance excess amount of 
equity received from MOR under BSRP from time to 
time shall be treated as Grant’s from MOR. Hence, it 
has been shown under Long Term Borrowings.
In the 110th Board Meeting it has been resolved to 
transfer of Assets & Liabilities with respect to BSRP 
project from K-RIDE to B-RIDE. The whole process will 
be completed within six months.
The necessary actions are under process.

In respect of Electrical Work (YPR-CHN) &Signaling Work

than the Estimated cost by Rs.195.21 lakhs and Rs. 1,063.05

cation No.03/2022 dt. 13.07.2022. This has resulted in the  
inadequate internal control system in getting revised e  
stimated cost on time.

was signed on 14.07.2022 i.e. FY 2022-23. However,

ways during the year 2018.

will be considered accordingly. 
2
As per Sl. No.6 of MOU dated 14th July 2022 relating Dou -
bling Project, the Project shall be completed within 36 
months from July,2020. To date, the project is not yet com-
pleted. There is no mutual consent in writing for having 
agreed for extension of period. This has resulted in the in -
adequate internal control system in getting the extension 
for completion of the project.
As per “Terms of Agreement” (Sl No.1) of MOU for  
Doubling, this MOU shall be valid till both the projects 
are completed in all aspect including the defect liability  
period / maintenance period and completion report is 
duly prepared and agreed by the parties. 
In view of the above, the MOU is still valid. However, mu-
tual consent of both the parties for extension of period 
will be obtained in the due course of time.

Doubling Project, the details of requirement of land to 
Railways after Final Location Survey are to be submitted. 
Details of land requirement for the two doubling projects 
have not been submitted. This may result in a delay in the 
completion of the project.
Final Location Survey has been done by Railways. There 
is no major land acquisition involved in the doubling 
projects.
However, if need for any land acquisition is arising, same 
is being processed on case-to-case basis.

Management’s Replies to the Observations of the Statutory Auditor on the  
Consolidated Financial Statement of K-RIDE for the year ended 31- March -2025
Sl.no. Basis of Qualified Opinion Management Replies
1
The Holding company is mainly involved in execution 
of Railway Line Doubling Projects and was operating 
the funds related to the same. However, during last 
two financial years the Holding Company started re -
ceiving funds from Government of Karnataka (Here -
inafter called GOK) as well as Ministry of Railways 
(Hereinafter called MOR) for implementation of Ban -
galore Sub-urban Railway Project (Herein after called 
BSRP Project). Again, as per directions received, the 
Holding company has established BRIDE as its sub -
sidiary to execute BSRP Project. Meanwhile the funds 
received from GOK and MOR towards BSRP Project; 
despite having created a subsidiary for execution of 
BSRP Project, the Holding company has converted 
the funds received from GOK and MOR into Share 
Capital of the Holding Company. Even though the 
Holding company is executing the BSRP Project, the 
discussions are going on to transfer entire BSRP funds 
and corresponding assets and liabilities to BRIDE. 
There is no clarity either from GOK or MOR regard -
ing this issue. By allotting shares to MOR, The Hold -
ing company has made MOR shareholder to the ex -
tent of 49% of its existing operations which was not 
warranted. This has resulted into doubt regarding 
continuity of BSRP Project Implementation through 
Holding company. This has a bearing on Accounting 
issues, Shareholding issues and Going Concern issues 
etc. In the absence of clarity, we are not in a position 
to quantify the Financial and other Implications on 
The Holding company.
Due to Administrative reason, K-RIDE continued its  
business with respect to implementation of Ben -
galuru Suburban Rail Project (BSRP)/Bengaluru  
suburban Transport Project (BSTP) and receiving 
the funds through respective Governments (i.e from 
GoK&MoR respectively) based on the funding pattern 
sanctioned by both the Governments. (i.e. 51% GoK& 
49% MoR)
However, in the 110th Board Meeting it has been re -
solved to transfer all Assets and Liabilities with respect 
to BSRP/BSTP Project from K-RIDE to Bi-RIDE which 
has been constituted for the exclusive purpose of  
implementation and management of BSRP/BSTP . The 
whole process will be completed within next Six-nine 
months. The necessary actions are under process.
Accordingly, the continuity of the BSRP business is not 
impacted, as the project along with all its related assets, 
liabilities, contractual obligations, rights, and responsi -
bilities is being transferred to Bi-RIDE as a going con -
cern, in accordance with the approval of the Board and 
the directions of the Government.
Till the completion of the transfer process, K-RIDE  
continues to discharge its obligations relating to BSRP/
BSTP and necessary accounting treatment shall be 
carried out in accordance with applicable accounting 
standards and statutory requirements.
Also, it is imperative to note that Doubling Projects will 
be continued to be executed by K-RIDE.
2
The Holding company is executing two Railway line 
doubling projects of Southwestern Railways (SWR 
in brief ). The Holding company is following percent-
age of completion method based on cost incurred  
method/ Input method under Ind AS for recogni -
tion of Revenue. Since The Holding company is  
using Input method, it is prudent to recognize Rev -
enue based on the estimated cost as on date. How -
ever, the Holding company is recognizing Revenue 
based on the approved cost dated 14-07-2022. As per 
MOU dated 14.07.2022 between The Holding compa-
ny and SWR any change in the DPR and the detailed 
estimate shall be sanctioned by SWR. The Holding 
company has submitted a detailed revised estimated 
cost based on current rates and duly approved by the 
technical team to MOR. Following are the differences 
between original estimates and revised estimates.
Revenue is recognized based on the approved  
sanctioned estimate (MOU signed on 14.07.2022 i.e.. 
FY 2022-23) & the same has been disclosed in notes to  
accounts. However, K-RIDE has worked out the revised 
estimate considering escalation costs and change in 
GST rate which has been submitted to SWR for approval. 
Further, after approval of SWR &GoK, the same will be 
considered accordingly.

3
The Net Profit for the reporting period is Rs. 1,027.05 lakhs, 
which includes Bank Interest income of Rs.1,577.52 Lakhs. 
The holding Company being an Implementing Agency, 
has to receive a fixed percentage of Supervision Charg -
es and cannot incur operational loss during the normal 
course of business. However, barring Interest Income, 
The Holding company has incurred a loss before taxes 
amounts Rs.550.47 lakhs. The Main reason for the loss is 
that The Holding company has submitted a detailed re -
vised Estimate duly approved from its Technical Team for 
approval before the Ministry of Railway Authorities. As on 
date of signing this report, the Ministry of Railways has not 
approved the revised estimate. This has resulted into Un -
derstatement of Revenue as well as Receivable Balance. In 
absence of complete information, we are not in a position 
to quantify the extent of Short Revenue and correspond -
ing Receivables value. This may also create Ind AS issues 
in subsequent financial years as Ind AS requires that any 
adjustments made in books corresponding to previous fi -
nancials years, then corresponding effect shall be given to 
those financial years and there will be a need to restate the 
previous financial years figures.
Revenue is recognized based on the approved sanc -
tioned estimate/DPR (MOU signed on 14.07.2022 i.e. 
FY 2022-23) & the same has been disclosed in notes to 
accounts. However, K-RIDE has worked out the revised 
estimate considering escalation costs and change in 
GST rate which has been submitted to SWR for approval. 
Further, after approval of SWR &GoK, the same will be 
considered accordingly. 
As disclosed in the financials note no.32(a), All the ex -
penses pertaining to BSRP including apportioned 
cost have been transferred to capital work in progress 
whereas, Doubling/ Admin & general overhead expens-
es are charged to P&L account. (i.e. Expenditure pertain-
ing to support departments such as Finance, HR, IT, Ad-
min & general overhead expenses are charged to P&L 
account.)
4
The Railway Line doubling project on specified routes are 
being undertaken by the Holding company in joint par -
ticipation between Ministry of Railways (MOR in brief ) 
and Government of Karnataka (GOK in brief ). Both MOR 
and GOK have contributed to the project in Equal Propor -
tion. The Modus operandi is that the Holding company will 
raise Invoice to Ministry of Railways as per agreed terms 
and conditions. Against these invoices the funds will have 
to be received both from MOR and GOK. Even though GOK 
is contributing 50% of its shares all the Billings by Hold -
ing company shall be made to MOR as per terms of agree-
ment. As per information provided to us the GOK will hand 
over money to Holding company directly. MOR will release 
its share as and when the Bills are raised by the Holding 
company. 
As a matter of prudence and to reflect the true and fair 
view of the affairs of The Holding company, The Holding 
company has to account for contract revenue as per the 
terms of the agreement entered between The Holding 
company, MOR and GOK. 
Funds will be released based on the Budget earmarked 
(for the particular year and availability of funds). How -
ever, it is to note that GoK has released only Rs.100 
Crore during the FY 2020-21 and no further funds made 
available from GoK for doubling projects. A continuous 
effort are being made by K-RIDE to get the necessary 
funds from GoK by communicating it through official 
letters sighting the shortage of funds.
As a temporary arrangement, K-RIDE has utilised the 
BSRP funds to the extent of Rs.171.89 Crore for Dou -
bling project to meet the work target and contractual 
obligations as both the projects are equally funded by 
GoK & MoR.
The same will be recouped to BSRP project on receipt of 
Doubling funds from GoK.

As each of the participants contribute 50% of the funds 
required for the project, ideally the receivables balance of 
The Holding company should reflect how much money is 
receivable from GOK as well as MOR. 
As can be seen from Accounts MOR has released its share as 
and when the bills are raised. However, no written Balance 
Confirmation is obtained from either GOK or MOR with 
respect to Contract Revenue Accounted, Funds Released 
and balance receivable. For KRIDE, MOR and GOK are like 
outside customers, and KRIDE is not bound by budget  
allocations of GOK. In the normal course of business,  
company has to account for revenue as per INDAS 115 
and the receivables from each of the customer has to be 
reflected in the books of accounts. As per the information 
provided by The Holding company, the status of work ex -
ecuted and funds received/receivable in respect of dou -
bling projects as at 31st March 2025 is as under:
In our view the MOR has released excess money, and 
the GOK has to pay its dues to KRIDE. It is also not clear 
whether the excess amount received from MOR is advance 
received or to be recognized as revenue. As per statement 
furnished the 
GOK has given 10000 Lakhs as on date and the balance  
receivable is Rs.25,336 Lakhs. Presuming, MOR has released 
Rs.8,147 Lakhs in excess, company has to show advance 
received from MOR and corresponding balance to be 
shown as 25,336 Lakhs. This has affected the true and fair  
presentation of the Accounts as Current assets have been 
understated by 25,336 Lakhs and Current liabilities have 
been understated by Rs.8,147 Lakhs.
The receivable will only be accounted upon issue of 
Government Order (GO) for release of funds. (since, the 
release of funds will depend on availability of funds with 
the Government and based on the different parameters 
even though the budget is earmarked).
Accordingly, no receivable shall be recognised mere -
ly on the basis of budget allocation; recognition will 
be made only when there is reasonable assurance  
evidenced by a GO sanctioning the release in the par -
ticular year.
5
Further since GOK has not paid its dues the amount pay -
able to contractor should have gone up to that extent. 
But on our verification, it was observed that the Funds re -
ceived from GOK in respect of BSRP project has been used 
to pay to the Contractors and as a result there is no out -
standing dues payable to Contractors.  In order to make 
payments to contractors out of earmarked funds for BSRP 
Projects, The Holding company management should have 
obtained prior permission from GOK as the funds are allo -
cated out of specified budgetary allocations from GOK and 
since it is approved in Karnataka legislative Assembly and 
project specific funds are received, there cannot be diver -
sion of funds from one project to another project. This has 
resulted into violation of financial powers vested with The  
Holding company resulting in diversion of funds with -
out written approval from GOK to the extent of Rs.25,336 
Lakhs.

7
The Holding company has not ascertained the liability for 
Property Tax under The Bruhat Bengaluru MahanagaraPa -
like Act, 2020 on lands acquired by The Holding company. 
During the year no provision for property tax is made by 
The Holding company. We are unable to quantify the im -
pact on profit.
This matter was also reported in previous audit report for 
the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
Property Tax can be assessed on completion of 
Katha transfer in the name of K-RIDE. The process of  
transferring property accounts of the acquired assets to 
K-RIDE is currently in progress. 
On completion of Katha transfer the Property Tax 
will be identified by Land department and necessary  
provisions will be made in books.
Due to a recent software update at the BBMP office, 
the process of account transfer has temporarily been  
 impacted, and further transfers are currently not  
possible. However, the remaining cases are under active 
processing, and all necessary steps are being taken to 
expedite khata registration.
In this regard, a letter dated 03.02.2025 has been  
issued by the Managing Director of K-RIDE to the Special  
Commissioner (Revenue), BBMP , requesting suitable 
modifications in the system to facilitate a smoother and 
faster khata transfer process in Favor of K-RIDE.
8
The Holding company is availing GST input tax credit in 
respect of direct expenditure relating to doubling project 
and is not availing Input Tax Credit relating to BSRP pro -
ject under the pretext that it is not entitled for credit as per 
provisions section 17(5) of the CGST/KGST Act. However, 
since the BSRP Project is going to be transferred to a sepa-
rate entity in our view The Holding company is Supplying 
Works Contract to Ministry of Railways and is incurring the 
Capital Expenditure in fiduciary capacity out of funds re -
ceived from MOR and GOK. Therefore, The Holding compa-
ny could have claimed Input Tax Credit. Further the input 
tax credit on common expenditure allocated to doubling 
projects is not claimed and the same is debited to Profit 
and Loss statement. In absence of information, we are un -
able to quantify the impact on profit. The Input in respect 
of BSRP Project amounts to Rs.3,365.57 Lakhs. 
This matter was also reported in previous audit report for 
the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
Currently K-RIDE is implementing two Doubling  
projects along with BSRP project. K-RIDE has claimed 
GST input specifically related to Doubling project and 
inputs related to BSRP project are not claimed since 
the same is blocked u/s 17(5) of CGST Act, 2017.  In -
puts related to BSRP projects along with the expens -
es have been included in the Capital WIP for which  
depreciation will be claimed as per provisions of section 
16(3) of CGST Act, 2017 as and when the asset is ready 
for its intended use.
K-RIDE has not claimed GST inputs on common expend-
iture allocated to doubling project based on the expert 
opinion taken from the Tax Consultant of K-RIDE.
9
The Holding company has shown the opening balance of 
Rs.16,541.82 Lakhs (net of transfer to share capital) as Inter-
est Free Subordinate Debt from GOK (Advance from GOK 
- BSRP Project-Land Acquisition). During the current finan-
cial year The Holding company has received an additional 
amount of Rs.59,000 Lakhs as an Advance from GOK - BSRP 
Project-Land Acquisition. As per letter dated 05-12-2024 
from GOK, it is mentioned that the funds of Rs.16,541.82 
Lakhs should be treated as the Subordinate debt. For the 
balance funds of Rs. Rs.59,000 Lakhs, no confirmation re -
ceived from GOK either to treat it as a Subordinate debt or 
grant-in-aid. 
As per the approved Financing pattern with respect to 
Bengaluru Suburban Rail Project (BSRP) in line with Gov-
ernment Order from GoK vide GO No. UDD 27 PRJ 2020, 
Bengaluru dated 07.07.2020 and Sanction order from 
Ministry of Railways (Railway Board) vide OM no.2019/
JV Cell/KRIDE/BSTP/EBR/07 dated 21.10.2020, the cost 
of the Land and R&R for BSRP has to be borne by GoK 
through Sub-ordinate Debt. 
Since, KRIDE has received the Funds on account of Land 
Acquisition, the same has been accounted in KRIDE’s 
Books of account as Sub-ordinate debt (Long Term Bor-
rowings) based on the sanctioned funding pattern.

[page 32]

Page         of 162
In our opinion the aforesaid amount of Rs.59,000 Lakhs 
relating to Advance from GOK for Land Acquisition shall 
be shown as Government Grants for Capital Expenditure, 
as per Ind AS 20- Deferred Government Grants Para 12 
and 13 relating to Accounting for Government Grants. 
This has resulted in understatement of Deferred Govern -
ment Grants and overstatement of Long-Term Borrowings 
by Rs.17,101.25 Lakhs and other equity by Rs.41,898.75 
Lakhs and also resulted in non-compliance to Division II of 
Schedule III to the Companies Act,2013.
10
The Holding company has received Rs.50,000 lakhs from 
MOR towards Share Application Money up till 31.03.2024. 
Out of the aggregate amount of Rs.50,000 lakhs, The Hold-
ing company has transferred Rs.48,039.22 lakhs to Share 
Capital during the financial year 2023-24 and the balance 
of Rs.1,960.79 Lakhs has shown as Grant from MOR – BSRP 
under Long Term Borrowings.
In our opinion, the aforesaid balance of Rs.1,960.79 Lakhs 
shall be shown as Share Application Money pending al -
lotment. This has resulted in understatement of Share Ap -
plication Money pending allotment and overstatement of 
Long-Term Borrowings by Rs. 1,960.79 Lakhs and also re -
sulted in non-compliance to Division II of Schedule III to 
the Companies Act,2013.
This matter was also reported in previous audit report for 
the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
In the 97th Board Meeting it was resolved that the al -
lotment of shares for equity investments under BSRP by 
GOK & MOR in K-RIDE shall be in ratio of 51:49 as per the 
stipulations contained in the AOA of K-RIDE & to avoid 
disproportionate shareholding for equity under BSRP . 
It was also resolved that the GOK’s 50% share of equi -
ty under BSRP released from time to time under BSRP 
shall be treated as 51% equity and the proportionate 
49% of MOR’s equity will be worked out from the 50% 
share of MOR’s equity under BSRP released from time to 
time and the balance excess amount of equity received 
from MOR under BSRP from time to time shall be treated 
as Grant’s from MOR. Hence, it has been shown under 
Long Term Borrowings.
In the 110th Board Meeting it has been resolved to 
transfer of Assets & Liabilities with respect to BSRP pro -
ject from K-RIDE to B-RIDE. The whole process will be 
completed within six months.
The necessary actions are under process.
11
The unspent amount of funds received from GOK and MOR 
have been invested by The Holding company in the form 
of Fixed Deposit in Banks. Interest earned to the extent of 
Rs.5166.00 lakhs (net amount) on such fixed deposits till 
31.03.2025 has been reduced from Capital work-in-pro -
gress as disclosed in Note No.3(d) of Notes to Accounts. 
The accounting treatment of grant as subordinate debt ap-
proved by the GOK to the extent of 16,541.82 lakhs and 
for the balance amount of Rs.59,000 lakhs The GOK is not 
yet approved the same. Further, as per the decision of 97th 
Board meeting the balance of share application money re-
ceived from MOR is treated as grants and the same is not 
yet approved by the MOR. Therefore, in the absence of cor-
rect nature of the funds, we are unable to ascertain the im-
pact of accounting treatment of the interest income which 
has been reduced from Capital work-in-progress.
This matter was also reported in previous audit report for 
the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
As per the approved Financing pattern with respect to 
Bengaluru Suburban Rail Project (BSRP) in line with Gov-
ernment Order from GoK vide GO No. UDD 27 PRJ 2020, 
Bengaluru dated 07.07.2020 and Sanction order from 
Ministry of Railways (Railway Board) vide OM no.2019/
JV Cell/KRIDE/BSTP/EBR/07 dated 21.10.2020, the cost 
of the Land and R&R for BSRP has to be borne by GoK 
through Sub-ordinate Debt. 
The amount of Rs.660 Crore has been approved by GoK 
to treat as subordinate debt. However, out of Rs.660 
Crore the amount of Rs.500 Crore was reclassified as Eq-
uity.
During the FY 2024-25 the FD interest earned is out of 
Equity Fund balance received towards BSRP project, 
hence the same is shown as Indirect Income in the 
Statement of P&L.

the construction of Compound wall. The Holding company 
has not ascertained the liability for the construction cost of 
Compound wall and no provision for the same is made by 
The Holding company. Therefore, we are unable to quan -
tify the impact on the cost of the land capitalized.
This matter was also reported in previous audit report for 
the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
The liability for construction cost of compound wall 
can be ascertained only after the construction work 
at particular said alignment work starts and based on 
the requirement at site, length of the compound wall 
requirement can be ascertained. Thus, the provision will 
be created after the construction work at particular said 
alignment work starts and on confirmation of the same 
from the Engineering department.

Management’s Replies to the Observations of the Statutory Auditor’s in the

Consolidated Financial Statement of K-RIDE for the year ended 31-March-2025
Sl.No. Basis of Qualified Opinion Management Replies
1
In respect of Electrical Work (YPR-CHN) &Signaling Work 
(BYPL-HSR) of doubling project undertaken by the holding 
company, actual expenses are more than the Estimated cost 
by Rs.195.21 lakhs and Rs. 1,063.05 lakhs respectively. The 
approval for the excess expenditure has not been obtained 
from the SWR. Further, no revised estimated cost has been 
obtained soon after the change in the GST rate from 12% to 
18% vide GOI Notification No.03/2022 dt. 13.07.2022. This 
has resulted in the inadequate internal control system in 
getting revised estimated cost on time.
As per the approved sanctioned estimate/DPR, MOU 
was signed on 14.07.2022 i.e. FY 2022-23. However, 
doubling work was transferred to K-RIDE based on 
the above approved estimate which was prepared by  
Railways during the year 2018. 
Currently, K-RIDE’s Executive section has worked out 
the revised estimate considering escalation costs and 
change in GST rate and submitted to SWR (MoR) for  
approval. Further, on approval of MoR&GoK the same 
will be considered accordingly. 
2
As per Sl. No.6 of MOU dated 14th July 2022 relating Dou -
bling Project undertaken by Holding Company, the Project 
shall be completed within 36 months from July,2020. To 
date, the project is not yet completed. There is no mutual 
consent in writing for having agreed for extension of peri -
od. This has resulted in the inadequate internal control sys-
tem in getting the extension for completion of the project.
As per “Terms of Agreement” (Sl No.1) of MOU for Dou -
bling, this MOU shall be valid till both the projects are 
completed in all aspect including the defect liability 
period / maintenance period and completion report is 
duly prepared and agreed by the parties. 
In view of the above, the MOU is still valid. However,  
mutual consent of both the parties for extension of pe -
riod will be obtained in the due course of time. 
3
As per Sl. No.14 of MOU dated 14th July 2022 relating to 
Doubling Project undertaken by Holding Company, the de-
tails of requirement of land to Railways after Final Location 
Survey are to be submitted. Details of land requirement for 
the two doubling projects have not been submitted. This 
may result in a delay in the completion of the project.
Final Location Survey has been done by Railways. There 
is no major land acquisition involved inthe doubling 
projects.  
However, if need for any land acquisition is arising, same 
is being processed on case-to-case basis. 
4
The Holding company has no proper control system to ob -
tain balance confirmation from the parties on a regular in -
terval basis.
Letter for confirmation of balances for balances as on 
31.03.2025 has been sent to all creditors with a note 
stating that “if the communication is not received with-
in one week from the receipt of this letter/e-mail, then 
KRIDE would treat the balance as stated is a deemed 
confirmation” .
5
We draw the attention to Para 4 of our report of even date, 
where the funds to the extent of Rs.25,336 Lakhs. have been 
diverted from BSRP Project to Railway doubling line project.
As a temporary arrangement, K-RIDE has utilised the 
BSRP funds for Doubling project to meet the work tar -
get and contractual obligations as both the projects are 
equally funded by GoK&MoR. 
The same will be recouped to BSRP project on receipt of 
Doubling funds from GoK.

b. Comptroller and Auditor General of India
 In terms of applicable provisions of Companies Act 2013, the Company is required take the comments on the financial 
statements of the Company issued by the Comptroller and Auditor General (C&AG) of India to every financial statement 
to be laid before the company in Annual General Meeting. After the completion of the Supplementary Audit, C&AG has 
issued its comments and also NIL report as required under Section 143(6) (b) to the Company and it is placed at the 24th 
Annual General Meeting of the company for kind noting of the members of the company. Copy of the Supplementary 
Audit Report of CAG has been attached as ANNEXURE - I.
c. Maintenance of Cost Records and Cost Audit
 Considering the business activities of the Company and based on the turnover of the immediately preceding financial 
year, the provisions of Section 148 are not applicable to the Company. Hence details are not offered.
d. Secretarial Auditor
 As the Company is falling within the provisions of Section 204 of the Companies Act, 2013 read with Rule 9 of Companies 
(Appointment and Remuneration and Managerial Personnel) Rules, 2014, with the approval of the Competent Authority                                               
Milind Kulkarni & Associates, PCS was appointed as Secretarial Auditor for the Financial Year 2024-25 and he has issued 
Secretarial Audit report for the Financial Year 2024-25 in Form No. MR 3 Copy of the same Secretarial Audit Report has 
been attached as ANNEXURE - II. Directors are considering the observations raised by Secretarial Audiutor and taking 
care of all compliances.
e. Frauds reported by auditor
 Pursuant to sub-section (12) of section 143 of the Act, auditor has not reported any frauds during financial year.

INDEPENDENT AUDITOR’S REPORT
To,
The Members of Rail Infrastructure Development Company (Karnataka) Limited.
Report on the audit of Ind AS Standalone Financial Statements.
Qualified Opinion
We have audited the accompanying Standalone Ind AS financial statements of Rail Infrastructure Development  
Company (Karnataka) Limited (“the Company”) (Hereinafter referred to as The KRIDE) , which comprise the Balance Sheet 
as at 31 March 2025, the Statement of Profit and Loss, the Statement of Cash Flows and the Statement of changes in Equity 
for the year then ended and notes to the financial statements, including a summary of Material accounting policies and other 
explanatory information. This report supersedes our Independent Auditor’s Report dated 27 October 2025, on the Ind AS 
standalone Financial Statements of the company for the year ended 31 march 2025, which has been revised in the light of 
observations arising from the audit by Comptroller & Auditor General of India.
In our opinion and to the best of our information and according to the explanations given to us, except for the  
effects of the matters described in the Basis for Qualified Opinion section of our report, the aforesaid Standalone financial  
statements give the information required by the Companies Act, 2013, in the manner so required and give a true and fair view in  
conformity with the Ind AS and accounting principles generally accepted in India, of the state of affairs of the Company as at 
March 31, 2025, and  profit, total comprehensive income, the changes in equity and its cash flows for the year ended on that 
date.
Basis for Qualified Opinion.
We conducted our audit in accordance with the Standards on Auditing (SAs) specified under section 143(10) of the  
Companies Act, 2013. Our responsibilities under those Standards are further described in the Auditor’s Responsibilities for the 
Audit of the Financial Statements section of our report. We are independent of the Company in accordance with the Code of 
Ethics issued by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) together with the ethical requirements that are relevant 
to our audit of the Ind AS financial statements under the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 and the Rules thereunder, 
and we have fulfilled our other ethical responsibilities in accordance with these requirements and the ICAI’s Code of Ethics. 
We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our qualified opinion.
Matters giving rise to Qualified Opinion
1. The KRIDE is mainly involved in execution of Railway Line Doubling Projects and was operating the funds  
related to the same. However, during last two financial years the Company started receiving funds from Government of  
Karnataka (Hereinafter called GOK) as well as Ministry of Railways (Hereinafter called MOR) for implementation of Bangalore  
Sub-urban Railway Project (Herein after called BSRP Project). Again, as per directions received, the KRIDE has established 
BRIDE as its subsidiary to execute BSRP Project. Meanwhile the funds received from GOK and MOR towards BSRP Project; 
despite having created a subsidiary for execution of BSRP Project, the company has converted the funds received from 
GOK and MOR into Share Capital of the Company. Even though the KRIDE is executing the BSRP Project, the discussions 
are going on to transfer entire BSRP funds and corresponding assets and liabilities to BRIDE. There is no clarity either from 
GOK or MOR regarding this issue. By allotting shares to MoR the Company has made MOR shareholder to the extent of 
49% of its existing operations which was not warranted. This has resulted into doubt regarding continuity of BSRP Project 
Implementation through KRIDE. This has a bearing on accounting issues, Shareholding issues and Going Concern issues 
etc. In the absence of clarity, we are not in a position to quantify the Financial and other Implications on the Company.
2. The Company is executing two Railway line doubling projects of Southwestern Railways (SWR in brief ). The  
company is following percentage of completion method based on cost incurred method/ Input method under Ind AS for  
recognition of Revenue. Since the company is using Input method, it is prudent to recognize Revenue based on the  
estimated cost as on date. However, the company is recognizing Revenue based on the approved cost dated  
14-07-2022. As per MOU dated 14.07.2022 between The Company and SWR any change in the DPR and the detailed  
estimate shall be sanctioned by SWR. The company has submitted a detailed revised estimated cost based on current 
rates and duly approved by the technical team to MOR. Following are the differences between original estimates and 
revised estimates. 
Auditor’s Report on Ind AS Standalone Financial Statements of Company Prepared in Accordance with 
a Fair Presentation Framework

[page 41]
24
TH
 ANNUAL REPORT 2024-25
41
Page         of 162
(Rs. In Lakhs)
Name of the Project Y eshwantpur-Channasandra Doubling Byappanahalli-Hosur Doubling
Original Estimation dated 14-07-2022 31,411 49,873
Revised Estimation submitted to MOR 
but not yet approved. 68,357 1,16,077
Difference -36,946 -66,204
 As per information provided to us, till date revised estimated cost is not approved by SWR. In the absence of revised 
approved estimated cost, we are unable to quantify the impact on Revenue and Profit thereon. 
 This matter was also reported in the previous audit report for the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
3. The Net Profit for the reporting period is Rs.1031.30 lakhs, which includes Bank Interest income of Rs.1,577.52 lakhs. 
Company being a Implementing Agency, has to receive a fixed percentage of Supervision Charges and cannot incur 
operational loss during the normal course of business. However, barring Interest Income, the company has incurred 
a loss before taxes amounting to Rs.546.22 lakhs. The Main reason for the loss is that the Company has submitted a  
detailed revised Estimate duly approved from its Technical Team for approval before the Ministry of Railway Authorities. 
As on date of signing this report, the Ministry of Railways has not approved the revised estimate. This has resulted into 
Understatement of Revenue as well as Receivable Balance. In absence of complete information, we are not in a posi -
tion to quantify the extent of Short Revenue and corresponding Receivables value. This may also create Ind AS issues in 
subsequent financial years as Ind AS requires that any adjustments made in books corresponding to previous financials 
years, then corresponding effect shall be given to those financial years and there will be a need to restate the previous 
financial years figures.
4. The Railway Line doubling project on specified routes are being undertaken by KRIDE in joint participation between 
Ministry of Railways (MOR in brief ) and Government of Karnataka (GOK in brief ). Both MOR and GOK have contributed 
to the project in Equal Proportion. The Modus operandi is that the KRIDE will raise Invoice to Ministry of Railways as per 
agreed terms and conditions. Against these invoices the funds will have to be received both from MOR and GOK. 
 Even though GOK is contributing 50% of its shares all the Billings by KRIDE shall be made to MOR as per terms of  
agreement. As per information provided to us the GOK will hand over money to KRIDE directly. MOR will release its share 
as and when the Bills are raised by the KRIDE. 
 As a matter of prudence and to reflect the true and fair view of the affairs of the company, the company has to account 
for contract revenue as per the terms of the agreement entered between the company, MOR and GOK. As each of the 
participants contribute 50% of the funds required for the project, ideally the receivables balance of the company should 
reflect how much money is receivable from GOK as well as MOR. 
 As can be seen from Accounts MOR has released its share as and when the bills are raised. However, no written Balance 
Confirmation is obtained from either GOK or MOR with respect to Contract Revenue Accounted, Funds Released and 
balance receivable. For KRIDE, MoR and GoK are like outside customers, and KRIDE is not bound by budget allocations of 
GOK. In the normal course of business, company has to account for revenue as per INDAS 115 and the receivables from 
each of the customer has to be reflected in the books of accounts. As per the information provided by the company, the 
status of work executed and funds received/receivable in respect of doubling projects as at 31st March 2025 is as under:
(
  in lakhs)
Doubling Estimate Expenditure till 31.03.2025 Amount released up to 31.03.2025 Shortage/ (Excess)
GoK 40,642 35,336 10000 25,336
MoR 40,642 35,336 43,483 -8,147
 81,283 70,672 53,483 17,189
 In our view the MOR has released excess money, and the GOK has to pay its dues to KRIDE. It is also not clear whether 
the excess amount received from MOR is advance received or to be recognized as revenue. As per statement furnished 
the  GOK has given 10000 Lakhs as on date and the balance receivable is Rs.25,336 Lakhs. Presuming, MOR has released 
Rs.8,147 Lakhs in excess, company has to show advance received from MOR and corresponding balance to be shown 
as Rs. 25,336 Lakhs. This has affected the true and fair presentation of the Accounts as Current assets have been under-
stated by Rs. 25,336 Lakhs and Current liabilities have been understated by Rs.8,147 Lakhs.
5. Further since GOK has not paid its dues the amount payable to contractor should have gone up to that extent. But on 
our verification, it was observed that the Funds received from GOK in respect of BSRP project has been used to pay 

[page 42]
24
TH
 ANNUAL REPORT 2024-25
42
Page         of 162
to the Contractors and as a result there is no outstanding dues payable to Contractors.  In order to make payments to  
contractors out of earmarked funds for BSRP Projects, the Company management should have obtained prior  
permission from GOK as the funds are allocated out of specified budgetary allocations from GOK and since it is approved in  
Karnataka legislative Assembly and project specific funds are received, there cannot be diversion of funds from one  
project to another project. This has resulted into violation of financial powers vested with the company resulting in  
diversion of funds without written approval from GOK to the extent of Rs.25,336 Lakhs. 
6. In the original cost estimate relating to doubling project undertaken by the Company, there are mistakes in estimates  
relating to individual type of work like Electrical, Signaling& Telecommunication and OHE. The net differences of Rs. 269.92 
lakhs have been added to total estimated cost. However, no changes have been affected on Direction & General charges 
on account of these additions to or deductions from the estimated cost. The exact amount of Direction & General charges 
mentioned in estimates relating to individual types of work like Electrical, Signaling& Telecommunication and OHE are 
only considered for recognizing the revenue. In connection with this matter, we are unable to quantify the impact on 
profit.
 This matter was also reported in previous audit report for the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
7. The Company has not ascertained the liability for Property Tax under The Bruhat Bengaluru MahanagaraPalike Act, 2020 
on lands acquired by the Company. During the year no provision for property tax is made by the Company. We are unable 
to quantify the impact on profit.
 This matter was also reported in previous audit report for the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
8. The Company is availing GST input tax credit in respect of direct expenditure relating to doubling project and is not  
availing Input Tax Credit relating to BSRP project under the pretext that it is not entitled for credit as per provisions 
section 17(5) of the CGST/KGST Act. However, since the BSRP Project is going to be transferred to a separate entity in 
our view the Company is Supplying Works Contract to Ministry of Railways and is incurring the Capital Expenditure in  
fiduciary capacity out of funds received from MoR and GoK. Therefore, the Company could have claimed Input Tax  
Credit. Further the input tax credit on common expenditure allocated to doubling projects is not claimed and the same is  
debited to Profit and Loss statement. In absence of information, we are unable to quantify the impact on profit. The Input 
in respect of BSRP Project amounts to Rs.3,365.57 Lakhs. 
 This matter was also reported in previous audit report for the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
9. The Company has shown the opening balance of Rs.16,541.82 Lakhs (net of transfer to share capital) as Interest Free 
Subordinate Debt from GOK (Advance from GOK - BSRP Project-Land Acquisition). During the current financial year 
the Company has received an additional amount of Rs.59,000 Lakhs as an Advance from GOK - BSRP Project-Land  
Acquisition. As per letter dated 05-12-2024 from GOK, it is mentioned that the funds of Rs.16,541.82 Lakhs should be 
treated as the Subordinate debt. For the balance funds of Rs. Rs.59,000 Lakhs, no confirmation received from GOK either 
to treat it as a Subordinate debt or grant-in-aid. 
 In our opinion the aforesaid amount of Rs.59,000 Lakhs relating to Advance from GOK for Land Acquisition shall be 
shown as Government Grants for Capital Expenditure, as per Ind AS 20- Deferred Government Grants Para 12 and 13 
relating to Accounting for Government Grants. This has resulted in understatement of Deferred Government Grants and  
overstatement of Long-Term Borrowings by Rs.17,101.25 Lakhs and other equity by Rs.41,898.75 Lakhs and also resulted 
in non-compliance to Division II of Schedule III to the Companies Act,2013. 
10. The Company has received Rs.50,000 lakhs from MoR towards Share Application Money up till 31.03.2024. Out of the  
aggregate amount of Rs.50,000 lakhs, The Company has transferred Rs.48,039.22 lakhs to Share Capital during the  
financial year 2023-24 and the balance of Rs.1,960.79 Lakhs has shown as Grant from MOR – BSRP under Long Term  
Borrowings.
 In our opinion, the aforesaid balance of Rs.1,960.79 Lakhs shall be shown as Share Application Money pending allotment. 
This has resulted in understatement of Share Application Money pending allotment and overstatement of Long-Term  
Borrowings by Rs. 1,960.79 Lakhs and also resulted in non-compliance to Division II of Schedule III to the Companies 
Act,2013.
 This matter was also reported in previous audit report for the year ended 31st March 2024 and remains unresolved.
11. The unspent amount of funds received from GOK and MOR have been invested by The Company in the form of Fixed 
Deposit in Banks. Interest earned to the extent of Rs.5166.00 lakhs (net amount) on such fixed deposits till 31.03.2025 has 
been reduced from Capital work-in-progress as disclosed in Note No.3(d) of Notes to Accounts. 
 The accounting treatment of grant as subordinate debt approved by the GOK to the extent of 16,541.82 lakhs and 
for the balance amount of Rs.59,000 lakhs The GOK is not yet approved the same. Further, as per the decision of 97th 
Board meeting the balance of share application money received from MOR is treated as grants and the same is not yet  
approved by the MOR. Therefore, in the absence of correct nature of the funds, we are unable to ascertain the impact of

We draw attention to the following matters in notes to the financial statements:
a. The title deeds in respect of lands acquired through KIADB amounting to Rs. 44,086.01 lakhs, land acquired from BDA

adjusted against the aforesaid advance amount as disclosed in explanatory note “c” of Note no 3 of Notes to accounts. 
Further, out of lands acquired from private parties through KIADB (under compulsory acquisition) as on 31.03.2025 to 
the extent of 1,91,593.21 sqmts which are yet to be registered in the name of the Company, lands measuring 3,776.13 
sqmts are under dispute.
 Our opinion is not modified in respect of the above matters.

Key Audit Matters
 Reporting of key audit matters as per SA 701 is applicable to the Listed Companies. As the Company is an Unlisted  
Company, reporting of Key Audit Matters is not applicable to this Company.
 Information other than Financial Statements and Auditor’s Report thereon
 The Company’s Board of Directors is responsible for the other information. The other information comprises the  
information included in the Annual Report but does not include financial statements and our auditor’s report thereon.
 Our opinion on the financial statements does not cover the other information and we do not express any form of  
assurance conclusion thereon.
 In connection with our audit of financial statements, our responsibility is to read the other information and, in doing 
so, consider whether the other information is materially inconsistent with the financial statements, or our knowledge is  
obtained in the audit or otherwise appears to be materially misstated.
 When we read other information, if we conclude that there is a material misstatement there in, we are required to  
communicate the matter to those charged with governance and take necessary actions, as applicable under the  
applicable laws and regulations. We have nothing to report in this regard.
 Our opinion is not modified in respect of these matters.
 Responsibilities of Management and Those Charged with Governance for the Standalone Ind AS Financial Statements
 The Company’s Board of Directors is responsible for the matters in section 134(5) of the Companies Act, 2013 (“the Act”) 
with respect to the preparation of these Ind AS standalone financial statements that give a true and fair view of the  
financial position, financial performance, including other comprehensive income, changes in equity and cash flows 
of the Company in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in India, including Indian Accounting  
Standards (Ind AS) prescribed under section 133 of the Act read with the Companies (Indian Accounting Standards) 
Rules, 2015, as amended. This responsibility also includes the maintenance of adequate accounting records in accordance 
with the provision of the Act for safeguarding of the assets of the Company and for preventing and detecting the frauds 
and other irregularities; selection and application of appropriate accounting policies; making judgments and estimates 
that are reasonable and prudent; and design, implementation and maintenance of internal financial control, that were  
operating effectively for ensuring the accuracy and completeness of the accounting records,   relevant to the preparation and  
presentation of the standalone Ind AS financial statements that give a true and fair view and are free from material  
misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.
 In preparing the Ind AS financial statements, management is responsible for assessing the Company’s ability to  
continue as a going concern, disclosing, as applicable, matters related to going concern and using the going concern  
basis of accounting unless management either intends to liquidate the Company or to cease operations, or has no realistic  
alternative but to do so.
 Those Board of Directors are also responsible for overseeing the company’s financial reporting process.
 Auditor’s Responsibilities for the Audit of Standalone Ind AS Financial Statement
 Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the Standalone Ind AS financial statements as a whole 
are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an auditor’s report that includes our  
opinion. Reasonable assurance is a high level of assurance but is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance 
with Standards on Auditing (SAs) will always detect a material misstatement when it exists. Misstatements can arise from 
fraud or error and are considered material if, individually or in aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to influence 
the economic decisions of users taken on the basis of these Standalone Ind AS financial statements.
 As part of an audit in accordance with Standards on Auditing (SAs), we exercise professional judgment and maintain  
professional skepticism throughout the audit. We also: 
•	 Identify	 and	 assess	 the	 risks	 of	 material	 misstatement	 of	 the	 financial	 statements,	whether	 due	 to	 fraud	 or	 error,	
design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain audit evidence that is sufficient and  
appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from 
fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions,  
misrepresentations, or the override of internal control. 
•	 Obtain	 an	 understanding	 of	 internal	 control	 relevant	 to	 the	 audit	 in	 order	 to	 design	 audit	 procedures	 that	 are	 
appropriate in the circumstances. Under section 143(3)(i) of the Companies Act, 2013, we are also responsible for 
expressing our opinion on whether the company has an adequate internal financial control with reference to stand 
alone financial statements in place and the operating effectiveness of such controls.
•	 Evaluate	 the	 appropriateness	 of	 accounting	 policies	 used	 and	 the	 reasonableness	 of	 accounting	 estimates	 and	 
related disclosures made by management.
